\par The first and second lines lead to a plus-regulated contribution which is straightforward to extract in Cartesian position space:
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{\pi^{3}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\\
\times e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot(\vec{}r_{\perp}+\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi)}\frac{[1-\xi(1-\xi)]^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\vec{}t_{\perp}}{t_{\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{\pi^{3}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\\
\times e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot(\vec{}r_{\perp}+\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi)}\frac{[1-\xi(1-\xi)]^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}}\cdot\frac{\vec{}t_{\perp}}{t_{\perp}^{2}}\par For the delta contribution, which comes from the first and third lines, we again need to use cutoff regularization. The position-dependent factors are
\begin{equation}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\Biggl[\frac{e^{-i\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}}{t_{\perp}^{2}}-\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\frac{e^{i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}}}{r_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}\Biggr]\end{equation}We'll first need to change variables $\vec{}s_{\perp}\to\vec{}r_{\perp}$ so that the Fourier factor $e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}$ doesn't change when we apply the delta function of $\vec{}t_{\perp}$, but otherwise, the procedure is the same as in section~\ref {sec:h14qq}. It gives two terms,
\begin{multline}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})-S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{0})\bigr]e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\frac{e^{-i\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}}{t_{\perp}^{2}}\\
-\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{0})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime}}\end{multline}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})-S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{0})\bigr]e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\frac{e^{-i\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}}{t_{\perp}^{2}}\\
-\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{0})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\frac{1}{2\pi}\ln\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime}}Then we need the integrals
\begin{multline}\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}\biggl[\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{(1-\xi^{\prime})_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr]e^{-i\xi^{\prime}x}\\
=i(e^{-ix}-1)\biggl(\frac{1}{x^{3}}-\frac{1}{x}\biggr)-\frac{e^{-ix}+1}{2x^{2}}+e^{-ix}[-\gamma_{E}+\Ci(x)-\ln(x)+i\Si(x)]\coloneqq\mathcal{I}_{3}(x)\end{multline}\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}\biggl[\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{(1-\xi^{\prime})_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr]e^{-i\xi^{\prime}x}\\
=i(e^{-ix}-1)\biggl(\frac{1}{x^{3}}-\frac{1}{x}\biggr)-\frac{e^{-ix}+1}{2x^{2}}+e^{-ix}[-\gamma_{E}+\Ci(x)-\ln(x)+i\Si(x)]\coloneqq\mathcal{I}_{3}(x)and
\begin{equation}\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}\biggl[\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{(1-\xi^{\prime})_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr]\ln\frac{1}{\xi^{\prime}}=-\frac{67}{72}+\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\end{equation}Putting everything together, we wind up with the quadrupole-type contribution
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{d6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{\pi^{3}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\Bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})-S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{0})\Bigr]\frac{1}{t_{\perp}^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\mathcal{I}_{3}(\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp})\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{d6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{\pi^{3}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\Bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})-S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{0})\Bigr]\frac{1}{t_{\perp}^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\mathcal{I}_{3}(\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp})and the dipole-type contribution
\begin{equation}F^{(1)}_{d2^{\prime\prime}gg}(z,1,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\biggl(-\frac{67}{36\pi^{2}}+\frac{1}{3}\biggr)\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{0})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\end{equation}which both go into
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{6gg}^{(1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}\frac{F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})-F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})}{1-\xi}\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}\biggl[F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})\ln\biggl(1-\frac{\tau}{z}\biggr)+F^{(1)}_{d6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})\biggr]\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}F^{(1)}_{d2^{\prime\prime}gg}(z,1,\vec{}r_{\perp})\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{6gg}^{(1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}\frac{F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})-F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})}{1-\xi}\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}\biggl[F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})\ln\biggl(1-\frac{\tau}{z}\biggr)+F^{(1)}_{d6gg}(z,1,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})\biggr]\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}F^{(1)}_{d2^{\prime\prime}gg}(z,1,\vec{}r_{\perp})\par\par To get the plus-regulated contribution in momentum space, we start with the expression from the first two lines of~\eqref{eq:xsec16gg}, which can be rewritten
\begin{multline}16\pi N_{c}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\frac{[1-\xi(1-\xi)]^{2}}{(1-\xi)_{+}}\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\\
\times\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}16\pi N_{c}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\frac{[1-\xi(1-\xi)]^{2}}{(1-\xi)_{+}}\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}\\
\times\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}We take the expression in the second line and insert delta functions as follows:
\begin{multline}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp}-\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime})\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime})\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp}-\vec{}r_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp})\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}{r_{\perp}^{\prime 2}t_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}\end{multline}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp}-\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime})\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime})\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp}-\vec{}r_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp})\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}{r_{\perp}^{\prime 2}t_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}This ``elevates'' $\vec{}s_{\perp}$ to an independent integration variable, rather than simply a shorthand for $\vec{}r_{\perp}+\vec{}t_{\perp}$. Next, we replace each of these delta functions with its Fourier transform according to equation~\eqref{eq:ident:deltaFT}, using $\vec{}q_{1\perp}$, $\vec{}q_{2\perp}$, and $\vec{}q_{3\perp}$ as the momentum variables, respectively. The resulting expression can be rearranged into
\begin{multline}(2\pi)^{2}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{3\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\Biggl(\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i(\vec{}k_{\perp}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{1\perp})\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\Biggr)\Biggl(\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})e^{i\vec{}q_{3\perp}\cdot\vec{}s_{\perp}}\Biggr)\\
\times\Biggl(\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i(\vec{}k_{\perp}/\xi+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp})\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\Biggr)\Biggl(\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}e^{-i\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}{r_{\perp}^{\prime 2}t_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}\Biggr)\end{multline}(2\pi)^{2}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{3\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\Biggl(\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i(\vec{}k_{\perp}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{1\perp})\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\Biggr)\Biggl(\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})e^{i\vec{}q_{3\perp}\cdot\vec{}s_{\perp}}\Biggr)\\
\times\Biggl(\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i(\vec{}k_{\perp}/\xi+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp})\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\Biggr)\Biggl(\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}e^{-i\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}{r_{\perp}^{\prime 2}t_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}\Biggr)The first three integrals in parentheses give factors of $\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}/S_{\perp}$ according to~\eqref{eq:dipoleFdefinition}, and the last is an application of~\eqref{eq:ident:vecprod}, so we wind up with
\begin{equation}-\frac{1}{(2\pi)^{2}}\frac{1}{S_{\perp}^{3}}\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{3\perp}\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}k_{\perp}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{1\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{3\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}}{\xi}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}\end{equation}Restoring the prefactors gives
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})=-\frac{4}{\pi}\frac{N_{c}}{S_{\perp}^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\frac{[1-\xi(1-\xi)]^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\\
\times\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}k_{\perp}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{1\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{3\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}}{\xi}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})=-\frac{4}{\pi}\frac{N_{c}}{S_{\perp}^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\frac{[1-\xi(1-\xi)]^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\\
\times\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}k_{\perp}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{1\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{3\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}}{\xi}+\vec{}q_{3\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}\par For the rest of the term, we use equation~\eqref{eq:ident:reglog} to turn the third line of~\eqref{eq:xsec16gg} into
\begin{multline}\delta(1-\xi)\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}\biggl[\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{(1-\xi^{\prime})_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr]\frac{1}{4\pi}\int\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\\
=\frac{\delta(1-\xi)}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}\int\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\biggl\{\biggl[\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{1-\xi^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr]\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\\
-\frac{1}{1-\xi^{\prime}}\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\biggr\}\end{multline}\delta(1-\xi)\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}\biggl[\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{(1-\xi^{\prime})_{+}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr]\frac{1}{4\pi}\int\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\\
=\frac{\delta(1-\xi)}{4\pi}\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}\int\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\biggl\{\biggl[\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{1-\xi^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr]\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\\
-\frac{1}{1-\xi^{\prime}}\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\biggr\}This basically takes the form $\iint\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}(\cdots)$. If we combine that with the other position-dependent factors from~\eqref{eq:xsec16gg}, we get
\begin{equation}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}s_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}t_{\perp})S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\iint\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}(\cdots)\end{equation}Inserting $\iint\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}\delta^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp}-\vec{}s_{\perp}+\vec{}t_{\perp})$ and expanding it with~\eqref{eq:ident:deltaFT}, with $\vec{}q_{1\perp}$ as the momentum variable, turns this into
\begin{equation}\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{1\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}k_{\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\vec{}k_{\perp})(\cdots)\end{equation}All in all, the delta contribution is
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{d6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\xi^{\prime})=-\frac{4N_{c}}{S_{\perp}^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(x_{p},\mu)}{z^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{1\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}k_{\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp})\\
\times\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\vec{}k_{\perp})\biggl[\biggl(\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{1-\xi^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr)\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}-\frac{1}{1-\xi^{\prime}}\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\biggr]\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{d6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\xi^{\prime})=-\frac{4N_{c}}{S_{\perp}^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(x_{p},\mu)}{z^{2}}\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{1\perp})\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}k_{\perp}-\vec{}q_{2\perp})\\
\times\mathcal{F}_{x_{g}}(\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\vec{}k_{\perp})\biggl[\biggl(\frac{\xi^{\prime}}{1-\xi^{\prime}}+\frac{1}{2}\xi^{\prime}(1-\xi^{\prime})\biggr)\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\xi^{\prime}\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}-\frac{1}{1-\xi^{\prime}}\ln\frac{(\vec{}q_{2\perp}-\vec{}k_{\perp})^{2}}{k_{\perp}^{2}}\biggr]This and equation~\eqref{eq:h16gg:momentum:Fs} go into~\eqref{eq:quadrupolemasterformulamomentum} in the form of
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{6gg}^{(1)}=\\
\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{3\perp}\biggl[\frac{F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})-F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})}{1-\xi}\biggr]\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{3\perp}F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})\ln\biggl(1-\frac{\tau}{z}\biggr)\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}F^{(1)}_{d4qq}(z,1,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\xi^{\prime})\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{6gg}^{(1)}=\\
\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{3\perp}\biggl[\frac{F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})-F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})}{1-\xi}\biggr]\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{3\perp}F^{(1)}_{s6gg}(z,1,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\vec{}q_{3\perp})\ln\biggl(1-\frac{\tau}{z}\biggr)\\
+\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}\int_{0}^{1}\udc\xi^{\prime}F^{(1)}_{d4qq}(z,1,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp},\xi^{\prime})\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\h{1,1}2gq$}{H(1,1)2gq}}
From equations (82) and (83) in~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Chirilli:2012jd}{}{}]}, we get this term to be
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2gq}^{(1,1)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}N_{c}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\\
\times\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{1}{\xi}\bigl[1+(1-\xi)^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2gq}^{(1,1)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}N_{c}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\\
\times\frac{1}{\xi^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{1}{\xi}\bigl[1+(1-\xi)^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}This has no plus-regulated or delta-function contribution, so the Cartesian position space representation is fairly trivial to find: we just compare the form of the term to equation~\eqref{eq:masterformula2} and find
\begin{equation}F^{(1,1)}_{n2gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{3}}\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{equation}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{12g}^{(1)}q=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}F^{(1,1)}_{n2gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})\end{equation}to produce the contribution to the cross section.
\par Because of the logarithmic factor, this can't be transformed into momentum space, but we can get the radial representation using equation~\eqref{eq:maptoradial}:
\begin{equation}F^{(1,1)}_{n2gq}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})J_{0}\biggl(\frac{k_{\perp}r_{\perp}}{\xi}\biggr)\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{3}}\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{equation}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{12g}^{(1)}q=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\udc r_{\perp}F^{(1,1)}_{n2gq}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})\end{equation}\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\h{1,2}2gq$}{H(1,2)2gq}}
Again from equations (82) and (83) in~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Chirilli:2012jd}{}{}]}
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2gq}^{(1,2)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}N_{c}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\\
\times\frac{1}{\xi}\bigl[1+(1-\xi)^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2gq}^{(1,2)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}N_{c}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\\
\times\frac{1}{\xi}\bigl[1+(1-\xi)^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}And like $\mathcal{H}^{(1,1)}_{2gq}$, this has only a normal contribution (no plus-regulated or delta-function terms), so again we can compare it to~\eqref{eq:masterformula2} to find
\begin{equation}F^{((1,2))}_{n2gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi}\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{equation}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{22g}^{(1)}q=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}F^{(1,2)}_{n2gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})\end{equation}\par The radial representation is easily obtained using~\eqref{eq:maptoradial}:
\begin{equation}F^{((1,2))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{N_{c}S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})J_{0}(k_{\perp}r_{\perp})\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi}\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{equation}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{22g}^{(1)}q=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\udc r_{\perp}F^{(1,2)}_{n2gq}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})\end{equation}Again, because of the logarithmic factor, there is no momentum-space representation.
\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\h 14gq$}{H(1)4gq}}
Equations (82) and (83) in~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Chirilli:2012jd}{}{}]} give us
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4gq}^{(1)}=4\pi N_{c}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\\
\times\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4gq}^{(1)}=4\pi N_{c}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)\\
\times\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}Again there is only a normal contribution, no plus-regulated or delta-function term, so the Cartesian position space representation of this term can be directly extracted from the last expression:
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi^{3}}N_{c}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi^{3}}N_{c}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}and that goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4gq}^{(1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}F^{(1)}_{n4gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})\end{equation}\par To convert this into momentum space, we rewrite the position-dependent parts of~\eqref{eq:xsec14gq} as
\begin{equation}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}\end{equation}and insert delta functions, in the form of~\eqref{eq:ident:deltaFT}, to get
\begin{multline}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\\
\times\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot(\vec{}s_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp}-\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime})}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot(\vec{}t_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime})}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}{r_{\perp}^{\prime 2}t_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}\end{multline}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}\\
\times\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot(\vec{}s_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp}-\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime})}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}e^{-i\vec{}q_{2\perp}\cdot(\vec{}t_{\perp}-\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime})}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}^{\prime}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}^{\prime}}{r_{\perp}^{\prime 2}t_{\perp}^{\prime 2}}which simplifies to
\begin{equation}-\frac{1}{S_{\perp}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\mathcal{G}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}}{\xi}+\vec{}q_{1\perp},\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}(\xi-1)}{\xi}-\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}\end{equation}The complete contribution is then
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4gq}^{(1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}F^{(1)}_{n4gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp})\end{equation}with
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp})=-\frac{1}{\pi}N_{c}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\\
\times\mathcal{G}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}}{\xi}+\vec{}q_{1\perp},\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}(\xi-1)}{\xi}-\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4gq}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp})=-\frac{1}{\pi}N_{c}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}q(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/g}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\frac{1+(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}\\
\times\mathcal{G}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}}{\xi}+\vec{}q_{1\perp},\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}(\xi-1)}{\xi}-\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\h{1,1}2qg$}{H(1,1)2qg}}
The $qg$ channel is very similar to the $gq$ channel. From equations (87) and (88) of~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Chirilli:2012jd}{}{}]} we get
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2qg}^{(1,1)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\\
\times\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]\biggl(\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}-1\biggr)\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2qg}^{(1,1)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\\
\times\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]\biggl(\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}-1\biggr)Again we compare it to~\eqref{eq:masterformula2} and find only a normal term:
\begin{multline}F^{((1,1))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{multline}F^{((1,1))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}in the Cartesian representation, which gets plugged into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2qg}^{(1,1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}F^{(1,1)}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})\end{equation}or
\begin{multline}F^{((1,1))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\\
\times J_{0}(k_{\perp}r_{\perp})\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{multline}F^{((1,1))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\\
\times J_{0}(k_{\perp}r_{\perp})\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2qg}^{(1,1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\udc r_{\perp}F^{(1,1)}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})\end{equation}in the radial representation. Again, the logarithmic factor precludes a momentum-space formula.
\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\h{1,2}2qg$}{H(1,2)2qg}}
From equations (87) and (88) of~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Chirilli:2012jd}{}{}]} we get
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2qg}^{(1,2)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]}{\xi^{2}}\biggl(\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}-1\biggr)\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{2qg}^{(1,2)}=\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{2}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}r_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\bigl[S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\bigr]^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{\bigl[(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}\bigr]}{\xi^{2}}\biggl(\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}-1\biggr)which again, has no plus-regulated or delta contribution. Comparing to~\eqref{eq:masterformula2} we find
\begin{multline}F^{((1,2))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\Bigl(S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\Bigr)^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}\biggl(\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}+1\biggr)\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{multline}F^{((1,2))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{8\pi^{2}}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\Bigl(S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\Bigr)^{2}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}/\xi}\biggl(\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}+1\biggr)\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{22q}^{(1)}g=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\udc r_{\perp}F^{(1,2)}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})\end{equation}for Cartesian position space and
\begin{multline}F^{((1,2))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\Bigl(S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\Bigr)^{2}J_{0}\biggl(\frac{k_{\perp}r_{\perp}}{\xi}\biggr)\biggl(\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}+1\biggr)\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}\end{multline}F^{((1,2))}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi}\frac{\alpha_{s}}{2\pi}\frac{S_{\perp}}{z^{2}}\sum_{i}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\Bigl(S_{x_{g}}^{(2)}(\vec{}r_{\perp})\Bigr)^{2}J_{0}\biggl(\frac{k_{\perp}r_{\perp}}{\xi}\biggr)\biggl(\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}}{\xi^{2}}+1\biggr)\ln\frac{c_{0}^{2}}{r_{\perp}^{2}\mu^{2}}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{22q}^{(1)}g=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\udc r_{\perp}F^{(1,2)}_{n2qg}(z,\xi,r_{\perp})\end{equation}for radial position space.
\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{\texorpdfstring{$\h 14qg$}{H(1)4qg}}
Equations (87) and (88) of~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Chirilli:2012jd}{}{}]} give us
\begin{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4qg}^{(1)}=4\pi S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}}{\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4qg}^{(1)}=4\pi S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\int\frac{\udc z\udc\xi}{z^{2}}\sum_{q}x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)\\
\times\int\frac{\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}}{(2\pi)^{4}}S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}}{\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}This is almost identical to $\mathcal{H}^{(1)}_{4gq}$, so the same procedure used in section~\ref {sec:h14gq} is applicable here. In Cartesian space, we get
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi^{3}}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}}{\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})=\frac{1}{4\pi^{3}}S_{\perp}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\\
\times S_{x_{g}}^{(4)}(\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}r_{\perp}}e^{-i\vec{}k_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}/\xi}\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}}{\xi}\frac{\vec{}r_{\perp}\cdot\vec{}t_{\perp}}{r_{\perp}^{2}t_{\perp}^{2}}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4qg}^{(1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}s_{\perp}\uddc\vec{}t_{\perp}F^{(1)}_{n4qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}s_{\perp},\vec{}t_{\perp})\end{equation}\par In momentum space, we get
\begin{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp})=-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}}{\xi}\\
\times\mathcal{G}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\vec{}k_{\perp}+\vec{}q_{1\perp},\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}(1-\xi)}{\xi}-\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}\end{multline}F^{(1)}_{n4qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp})=-\frac{1}{\pi}\frac{\alpha_{\text{s}}}{2\pi}\sum_{q}\frac{x_{p}g(x_{p},\mu)D_{\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}/q}(z,\mu)}{z^{2}}\frac{(1-\xi)^{2}+\xi^{2}}{\xi}\\
\times\mathcal{G}_{x_{g}}\biggl(\vec{}k_{\perp}+\vec{}q_{1\perp},\frac{\vec{}k_{\perp}(1-\xi)}{\xi}-\vec{}q_{1\perp}+\vec{}q_{2\perp}\biggr)\frac{\vec{}q_{1\perp}\cdot\vec{}q_{2\perp}}{q_{1\perp}^{2}q_{2\perp}^{2}}which goes into
\begin{equation}\biggl(\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}\biggr)_{4qg}^{(1)}=\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\int_{\tau/z}^{1}\udc\xi\int\uddc\vec{}q_{1\perp}\uddc\vec{}q_{2\perp}F^{(1)}_{n4qg}(z,\xi,\vec{}q_{1\perp},\vec{}q_{2\perp})\end{equation}\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{section}{toc}{Integration Methods}
\par The analytic formulas derived in the previous section, indexed in table~\ref {tbl:hardfactorformulas}, are numerically well-behaved and thus straightforward to implement in a computer program.
In a full calculation, SOLO integrates over anywhere from one variable, as in equation~\eqref{eq:h02qq:momentum:xsec}, to eight, in~\eqref{eq:h16gg:momentum:xsec}.
We'll need to use different integration algorithms to handle different kinds of integrals.
\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Cubature}
\par The most straightforward method of integration is called multidimensional quadrature, or cubature.
There are a tremendous number of variations on this method, and a full overview would be beyond the scope of this dissertation,
but the core of the method is as follows.
Given a one-dimensional function $f(x)$ to be integrated over an interval $[a,b]$, we choose $N$ points $x_{i}\in[a,b]$, called {\it abcissas}, evaluate the function at these points, and approximate the integral as
\begin{equation}\int_{a}^{b}f(x)\udc x\approx\sum_{i}w_{i}f(x_{i})\end{equation}where $w_{i}$ are a set of weights chosen to correspond to the abcissas.
Simple examples of this method include the trapezoidal rule,
\@@amsalign
and Simpson's rule (valid for $N$ odd),
\@@amsalign
These methods fall into a class that one might call polynomial-based integration rules, because they are derived by fitting polynomials (linear for the trapezoidal rule, quadratic for Simpson's rule) to successive sample points.
For reasonably well-behaved functions, one can estimate that the error in these approximations scales as $N^{-k}$ for some $k$, e.g. $k=2$ for the trapezoidal rule or $k=4$ for Simpson's rule.
\par There are also a variety of more complex algorithms, in particular the several variants of Gaussian quadrature, which use nonuniformly spaced abcissas to obtain exponential convergence --- that is, the error estimate scales as $a^{-N}$.
Reference~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{NumericalRecipes}{}{}]} contains a good overview of these methods.
\par Cubature methods can be applied to multidimensional integration by simply performing each integral in succession.
\begin{equation}\idotsint f(\vec{x})\udc^{d}\vec{x}=\int_{a_{0}}^{b_{0}}\Biggl(\int_{a_{1}(x_{0})}^{b_{1}(x_{0})}\cdots\Biggl(\int_{a_{d}(x_{0},\ldots,x_{d-1})}^{b_{d}(x_{0},\ldots,x_{d-1})}f(\vec{x})\udc x_{d}\Biggr)\cdots\udc x_{1}\Biggr)\udc x_{0}\end{equation}For SOLO we use an adaptive multidimensional cubature implementation~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{adaptint}{}{}]} based on the algorithms described in references~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{VanDooren1976,Berntsen1991}{}{}]}.
\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Monte Carlo}
\par Since multidimensional cubature performs an inner integral at every point needed to evaluate the outer integral, its runtime scales exponentially in the number of dimensions, which makes it unsuitable for anything beyond a 2D or maybe 3D integration region.
For higher-dimensional integrals, we turn to Monte Carlo integration.
This takes a random sampling of points throughout a $d$-dimensional integration region and uses them to estimate the value of the integral.
\par Consider a function of one variable.
There are two ways to think of Monte Carlo integration.
One way is to choose a rectangle $[a,b]\times[0,f_{\text{max}}]$, pick $N$ uniformly distributed random points $(x,y)$ in this rectangle, and count how many fall under the function.
The integral can then be approximated as the fraction of these points which fall below the function, times the area of the rectangle.
\begin{equation}\int_{a}^{b}f(x)\udc x\approx\frac{(b-a)f_{\text{max}}}{N}\sum_{i}\begin{cases}1,&y\leq f(x_{i})\\
0,&y>f(x_{i})\end{cases}\end{equation}But a more efficient way is to pick $N$ random values in $[a,b]$, evaluate the function at each, and compute the average.
We can assume the average of the $N$ values approximates the average of the function.
\begin{equation}\int_{a}^{b}f(x)\udc x\approx\frac{b-a}{N}\sum_{i}f(x_{i})=(b-a)\Braket{f(x)}\end{equation}where the angle brackets here indicate the average of $f(x)$ over all sample points $x_{i}$.
The theoretical standard deviation of this estimate is
\begin{equation}(b-a)\sqrt{\frac{\Braket{[f(x)]^{2}}-\Braket{f(x)}^{2}}{N}}\end{equation}This generalizes to multidimensional integrals easily:
\begin{equation}\int_{V}f(\vec{x})\udc^{d}\vec{x}\approx\frac{V}{N}\sum f(\vec{x}_{i})=V\Braket{f(\vec{x})}\end{equation}with theoretical standard deviation
\begin{equation}V\sqrt{\frac{\Braket{[f(x)]^{2}}-\Braket{f(x)}^{2}}{N}}\end{equation}The appeal of Monte Carlo integration is that the standard deviation is proportional to $1/\sqrt{N}$ regardless of the number of dimensions, as opposed to cubature, where the standard deviation scales as $N^{-d}$.
For high-dimensional integrals, Monte Carlo generally yields better results for a given number of sample points.
\par In most applications of Monte Carlo integration, including SOLO, the function being integrated ($f$) has a large magnitude in some parts of the integration region and is relatively close to zero elsewhere.
Clearly, most of the value of the integral comes from the regions where the integrand's magnitude is large.
If, instead of choosing sample points from a uniform random distribution, we can sample adaptively --- that is, choose more samples from these regions where the integrand is large --- it will improve the accuracy of the final integral.
A mathematical derivation~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{NumericalRecipes}{}{}, sec. 7.9.1]} shows that the optimal choice of the sampling distribution is
\begin{equation}p(\vec{x})=\frac{\abs{f(\vec{x}}}{\int\abs{f(\vec{x})}\udc^{d}\vec{x}}\end{equation}Choosing sample points with this probability distribution
This seems circular, because knowing $p$ requires knowing the integral $\int\abs{f(\vec{x})}\udc^{d}\vec{x}$ which we are trying to compute, but what we can do in practice is perform a quick Monte Carlo integration with uniform sampling to get an estimate of the integral, and then use~\eqref{eq:importancesamplingprobability} to choose more samples adaptively.
This technique is called {\it importance sampling}, and it forms the basis of the VEGAS adaptive Monte Carlo algorithm~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Lepage:1977sw,Lepage:1980dq}{}{}]}.
VEGAS was developed for applications in particle physics, so it is not surprising that it is particularly well suited for use in SOLO.
Experimentation has shown that the VEGAS algorithm gives by far the best accuracy for the computations.
\par\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{subsection}{toc}{Fixing the Domain of Integration}
The integration region over $\xi$ (or $x_{p}$) and $z$ is bounded by kinematics: $\frac{\tau}{z}\leq\xi,x_{p}\leq 1$ and $\tau\leq z\leq 1$, resulting in the shape shown in figure~\ref {fig:integrationdomain}. However, the C++ implementation uses integration routines from the GNU Scientific Library (GSL)~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{GSL}{}{}]}, either Monte Carlo or cubature, which can only handle simple rectangular integration domains with constants as limits.
\par Because all terms in equation~\eqref{eq:contributions} involve an integration over $z$ with constant limits $[\tau,1]$, it simplifies the code to use that as one of the variables of integration. The other variable needs to be chosen so that the integration region is a rectangle. The most straightforward way to do that is with a simple rescaling:
\begin{equation}\frac{y-y_{\text{min}}}{y_{\text{max}}-y_{\text{min}}}=\frac{\xi-\xi_{\text{min}}}{\xi_{\text{max}}-\xi_{\text{min}}}\end{equation}In this case, setting $y_{\text{min}}=\tau$, $\xi_{\text{min}}=\frac{\tau}{z}$, and $y_{\text{max}}=\xi_{\text{max}}=1$, we get
\begin{equation}y=\frac{z\xi(1-\tau)+\tau(z-1)}{z-\tau}\qquad\text{and its inverse}\qquad\xi=\frac{y(z-\tau)-\tau(z-1)}{z(1-\tau)}\end{equation}The accompanying Jacobian is
\begin{equation}\udc z\udc\xi=\begin{vmatrix}\pd{z}{z}&\pd{z}{y}\\
\pd{\xi}{z}&\pd{\xi}{y}\end{vmatrix}\udc z\udc y=\begin{vmatrix}1&\frac{(z-\tau)^{2}}{(1-\xi)(1-\tau)\tau}\\
0&\frac{z-\tau}{z(1-\tau)}\end{vmatrix}\udc z\udc y=\frac{z-\tau}{z(1-\tau)}\udc z\udc y\end{equation}giving an overall transformation of
\begin{equation}\int_{\tau}^{1}\int_{\frac{\tau}{z}}^{1}\frac{\udc z}{z^{2}}\frac{\udc x_{p}}{x_{p}}\xi(\ldots)=\int_{\tau}^{1}\int_{\tau}^{1}\udc z\udc y\frac{z-\tau}{z^{3}(1-\tau)}(\cdots)\end{equation}This extra factor in the Jacobian is the last addition we need to produce the numerical results that will be shown in the next chapter.
\par\lx@frontmatter@fallback\@@unnumbered@section{chapter}{toc}{Computational Results and Analysis}
\par After the algebraic manipulations of chapter~\ref {ch:crosssection}, we have an expression for the $\mathrm{p}A\to hX$ cross section, complete up to next-to-leading order, and free of both divergences and analytic singularities. We've developed a program called SOLO, Saturation at One-Loop Order~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{SOLO}{}{}]}, to implement the calculation.
\par Our first results from SOLO, published in reference~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Stasto:2013cha}{}{}]}, correspond to the experimental data collected at RHIC by the BRAHMS~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Arsene:2004ux}{}{}]} and STAR~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Adams:2006uz}{}{}]} experiments.
Updated versions of these results are shown in figures~\ref {fig:brahmsresults} and~\ref {fig:starresults}.
It's immediately evident from these plots that something interesting is going on: at high momenta, the LO+NLO cross section becomes negative!
\par\begin{figure}{1}
{1}
\hbox{\includegraphics[]{generated-figures/brahmsresults}}

The edges of the solid bands were computed using $\mu^{2}=\text{\SIrange{10}{50}{GeV^2}}$. \end{figure}\par\begin{figure}\hbox{\includegraphics[]{generated-figures/starresults}}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{29}}{Calculation results and data for $\mathit{{d}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{A}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\HepTo\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{X}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}$ cross section at STAR}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{\small Figure 29}}{\small Comparisons of STAR \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Adams:2006uz}{}{}]} ($\pi^{0}$) yields in $\mathrm{dAu}${} collisions to results of the numerical calculation with the rcBK gluon distribution, both at leading order (tree level) and with NLO corrections included. The edges of the solid bands were computed using $\mu^{2}=\text{\SIrange{10}{50}{GeV^2}}$. This is an updated version of figure 1 of~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Stasto:2013cha}{}{}]} with reduced statistical errors.
}}}
\end{figure}\par Needless to say, this was quite a surprising result.
Physically, of course, it's impossible for a cross section to be negative, so at first glance the output from SOLO appears completely nonsensical.
But it's actually not that unreasonable for a fixed-order perturbative result such as this one to be negative.
The true cross section is a sum of terms at all orders in $\alpha_{\text{s}}$,
\begin{equation}\frac{\udddc\sigma}{\udc Y\uddc\vec{p}_{\perp}}=(\text{LO})+\alpha_{\text{s}}(\text{NLO})+\alpha_{\text{s}}^{2}(\text{NNLO})+\cdots\end{equation}and it's only the final sum that corresponds to the physical cross section and thus has to be positive.
Negative contributions from the NLO term could be compensated by large positive contributions from further terms in the series.
\par In fact, previous work by Albacete et al.~\cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Albacete:2012xq}{}{}, fig. 9]} has already shown a trend toward negative results at high $p_{\perp}$ when some of the next-to-leading order contributions are included.
With two independent implementations both showing the same feature, we can be reasonably confident that we're looking at a characteristic of the NLO formulas, not a quirk of the program.\par At this point, there are several questions:
\begin{itemize}\par\itemize@item@Does the negativity imply anything about the validity of the small-$x$ formalism?
\par\itemize@item@What is the significance of the cutoff momentum at which the cross section turns negative?
\par\itemize@item@What benefit, if any, do we get by incorporating the NLO correction?
\par\itemize@item@Does the LO+NLO formula give an accurate result for any kinematic conditions?
\par\itemize@item@And the largest question: what can we meaningfully learn about the gluon distribution from the predictions of SOLO?
\par\end{itemize}\par\begin{figure}\hbox{\includegraphics[]{generated-figures/brahmsppresults}}
\@@toccaption{{\lx@tag[ ]{{30}}{Calculation results and data for $\mathup{{{p}}}\mathup{{{p}}}\HepTo\mathit{{h}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}\mathit{{X}{}_{\mspace{-3mu}\scriptstyle{}}^{\mspace{-1mu}\scriptstyle{}}}$ cross section at BRAHMS}}}\@@caption{{\lx@tag[: ]{{\small Figure 30}}{\small Comparisons of BRAHMS \cite[cite]{[\@@bibref{}{Arsene:2004ux}{}{}]} ($h^{-}$) yields in $\mathrm{pp}${} collisions to results of the numerical calculation with the rcBK gluon distribution, both at leading order (tree level) and with NLO corrections included. The edges of the solid bands were computed using $\mu^{2}=\text{\SIrange{10}{50}{GeV^2}}$. Here the calculation completely breaks down at $\eta=2.2$. This is not \emph{entirely} surprising, though, because a proton does not have the extremely high gluon densities of a large atomic nucleus, and thus the CGC model cannot be expected to perform as well.}}}
\end{figure}\par\begin{figure}\hbox{\includegraphics[]{generated-figures/starppresults}}

\end{figure}\par\begin{figure}\hbox{\includegraphics[]{generated-figures/brahmsRdAu}}
